### Download eventwatch.c for local installation example

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/c

This set of commands creates a new directory for a CMake project that uses a locally installed SDK and downloads the 'eventwatch.c' source file from a GitHub URL. This prepares the environment for compiling 'eventwatch.c' using CMake's find_package functionality, pointing to the previously installed SDK.

```bash
$ mkdir ~/src/event-sdk-example-c-find-package
$ cd ~/src/event-sdk-example-c-find-package
$ curl -O https://raw.githubusercontent.com/category-labs/monad/refs/heads/release/exec-events-sdk-v1.x/category/event/example/eventwatch.c
```

--------------------------------

### Install Monad Event SDK locally with CMake

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/c

This sequence of CMake and shell commands installs the Monad Event SDK ('libmonad_event.a') to a specified local directory ('/tmp/sdk-install-demo'). It first clones the SDK repository, then configures the build for the event category with Ninja, setting the installation prefix and build type. Finally, it builds the SDK and installs it.

```bash
$ git clone -b release/exec-events-sdk-v1.x https://github.com/category-labs/monad.git \
  ~/src/monad-exec-events-sdk
$ cmake -S ~/src/monad-exec-events-sdk/category/event \
  -B ~/build/monad-exec-events-sdk-v1-release -G Ninja \
  -DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX=/tmp/sdk-install-demo -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo
$ cmake --build ~/build/monad-exec-events-sdk-v1-release
$ cmake --install ~/build/monad-exec-events-sdk-v1-release
```

### JavaScript: Real-time Data Streaming Example

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/reference/websockets

Illustrates how a JavaScript library might abstract away the underlying Geth real-time events protocol. It focuses on presenting real-time data through a different API design, without explicit mention of subscription names like 'newHeads'.

```javascript
import { Web3 } from 'web3';

const web3 = new Web3('ws://localhost:8546');

web3.eth.subscribe('newHeads', (error, result) => {
    if (error) {
        console.error(error);
    } else {
        console.log(result);
    }
});
```

### Fetch Monad Event SDK via FetchContent (CMake)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/c

This CMake snippet demonstrates how to declare and make available the execution events C SDK directly from a Git repository using FetchContent. It specifies the repository, tag, shallow clone, exclusion of submodules, and a subdirectory for the top-level CMakeLists.txt to build only the 'monad_event' library. It also disables the SDK's example to prevent naming conflicts.

```cmake
include(FetchContent)


FetchContent_Declare(exec_events_c_sdk
    GIT_REPOSITORY git@github.com:category-labs/monad.git
    GIT_TAG release/exec-events-sdk-v1.x
    GIT_SHALLOW TRUE
    GIT_SUBMODULES ""
    SOURCE_SUBDIR category/event)


set(MONAD_EVENT_BUILD_EXAMPLE OFF CACHE INTERNAL "")


FetchContent_MakeAvailable(exec_events_c_sdk)


add_executable(eventwatch eventwatch.c)
target_compile_options(eventwatch PRIVATE -Wall -Wextra -Wconversion -Werror)
target_link_libraries(eventwatch PRIVATE monad_event)
```

### Build and Run eventcap using clang-19 and libc++

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/final

Steps to clone the Monad repository, configure the CMake build for `eventcap` using clang-19 with specific CFLAGS and CXXFLAGS (including libc++ and `-fexperimental-library`), and build the project.

```shell
$ git clone -b release/exec-events-sdk-v1.x https://github.com/category-labs/monad.git \
  ~/src/monad-eventcap
$ CC=clang-19 CFLAGS="-march=x86-64-v4" \
  CXX=clang++-19 CXXFLAGS="-stdlib=libc++ -fexperimental-library -march=x86-64-v4" cmake \
  -S ~/src/monad-eventcap/cmd/eventcap -B ~/build/monad-eventcap-release -G Ninja \
  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo
$ cmake --build ~/build/monad-eventcap-release
```

### CMakeLists.txt Configuration for Eventwatch

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/c

This CMakeLists.txt file configures the build for the 'eventwatch' executable. It specifies the minimum CMake version, project name, finds the required monad_exec_events_sdk package, defines the executable, sets compile options, and links the monad_event library.

```cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)


project(eventwatch LANGUAGES C)


find_package(monad_exec_events_sdk REQUIRED
             PATHS /tmp/sdk-install-demo/lib/cmake/category-labs)


add_executable(eventwatch eventwatch.c)
target_compile_options(eventwatch PRIVATE -Wall -Wextra -Wconversion -Werror)
target_link_libraries(eventwatch PRIVATE monad_event)

```

### Get Validator Sets (Paginated)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Fetches lists of validator IDs for consensus, snapshot, and execution contexts in a paginated manner. Each call returns a subset of validator IDs along with pagination controls (isDone, nextIndex), allowing efficient retrieval of large validator sets. The `startIndex` parameter controls the starting point of the retrieval.

```Solidity
function getConsensusValidatorSet(
    uint32 startIndex
) external returns (bool isDone, uint32 nextIndex, uint64[] memory valIds);
```

```Solidity
function getSnapshotValidatorSet(
    uint32 startIndex
) external returns (bool isDone, uint32 nextIndex, uint64[] memory valIds);
```

```Solidity
function getExecutionValidatorSet(
    uint32 startIndex
) external returns (bool isDone, uint32 nextIndex, uint64[] memory valIds);
```

### Configure Rust project dependencies in Cargo.toml

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/rust

Defines the project's metadata and dependencies, including the Monad execution event SDK packages sourced directly from a Git repository.

```toml
[package]
name = "event-sdk-example-rust"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2021"


[dependencies]
chrono = "0.4.34"
clap = { version = "4.2", features = ["derive"] }
lazy_static = "1.5.0"


[dependencies.monad-exec-events]
git = "https://github.com/category-labs/monad-bft"
branch = "release/exec-events-sdk-v1.x"

### Build eventwatch with CMake and make

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/c

This code block shows the command-line execution of CMake to configure the build for the 'eventwatch' program using the default compiler and the 'make' build system. It assumes the source code is located at '~/src/event-sdk-example-c' and the build output will be in '~/src/event-sdk-example-c/build'. After configuration, it navigates into the build directory and invokes 'make' to compile the project.

```bash
$ cmake -S ~/src/event-sdk-example-c -B ~/src/event-sdk-example-c/build
$ cd ~/src/event-sdk-example-c/build
$ make
```

### CMake Toolchain File for clang-19 and libc++

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/getting-started/final

Contents of a CMake toolchain file (`clang19-libcxx.cmake`) to simplify the build process for `eventcap` when using clang-19, libc++, and specific C++ standard library flags.

```cmake
set(CMAKE_C_COMPILER clang-19)
set(CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER clang++-19)
set(CMAKE_ASM_FLAGS_INIT -march=x86-64-v4)
set(CMAKE_C_FLAGS_INIT -march=x86-64-v4)
set(CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_INIT "-march=x86-64-v4 -stdlib=libc++ -fexperimental-library")
```

### Example: Adding shMON Token Configuration

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/templates/next-serwist-0x-privy-embedded-wallet

This example provides the concrete implementation for adding the 'shMON' token to the Monad testnet token configurations. It shows the correct structure for the `MONAD_TESTNET_TOKENS` array, the `MONAD_TESTNET_TOKENS_BY_SYMBOL` record (using 'shmon' as the key), and the `MONAD_TESTNET_TOKENS_BY_ADDRESS` record (using the lowercase contract address as the key).

```typescript
// In MONAD_TESTNET_TOKENS array
{
  chainId: 1,
  name: "shMonad",
  symbol: "shMON",
  decimals: 18,
  address: "0x3a98250F98Dd388C211206983453837C8365BDc1",
  logoURI: "put_your_logo_url_here_or_use_the_default_logo",
},


// In MONAD_TESTNET_TOKENS_BY_SYMBOL record
shmon: {
  chainId: 1,
  name: "shMonad",
  symbol: "shMON",
  decimals: 18,
  address: "0x3a98250F98Dd388C211206983453837C8365BDc1",
  logoURI: "put_your_logo_url_here_or_use_the_default_logo",
},


// In MONAD_TESTNET_TOKENS_BY_ADDRESS record
"0x3a98250F98Dd388C211206983453837C8365BDc1": {
  chainId: 1,
  name: "shMonad",
  symbol: "shMON",
  decimals: 18,
  address: "0x3a98250F98Dd388C211206983453837C8365BDc1",
  logoURI: "put_your_logo_url_here_or_use_the_default_logo",
},
```

### GET Endpoint: Blink Metadata and UI Configuration (TypeScript)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/blinks-guide

Defines the GET endpoint for a Blink action, returning metadata and UI configuration. It specifies how the action appears in clients, required parameters, and execution details. Dependencies include '@solana/actions'.

```typescript
import {
  ActionGetResponse,
} from "@solana/actions";


// GET endpoint returns the Blink metadata (JSON) and UI configuration
export const GET = async (req: Request) => {
  // This JSON is used to render the Blink UI
  const response: ActionGetResponse = {
    type: "action",
    icon: `${new URL("/donate-mon.png", req.url).toString()}`,
    label: "1 MON",
    title: "Donate MON",
    description:
      "This Blink demonstrates how to donate MON on the Monad blockchain. It is a part of the official Blink Starter Guides by Dialect Labs.  \n\nLearn how to build this Blink: https://dialect.to/docs/guides/donate-mon",
    // Links is used if you have multiple actions or if you need more than one params
    links: {
      actions: [
        {
          // Defines this as a blockchain transaction
          type: "transaction",
          label: "0.01 MON",
          // This is the endpoint for the POST request
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.01`,
        },
        {
          type: "transaction",
          label: "0.05 MON",
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.05`,
        },
        {
          type: "transaction",
          label: "0.1 MON",
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.1`,
        },
        {
          // Example for a custom input field
          type: "transaction",
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount={amount}`,
          label: "Donate",
          parameters: [
            {
              name: "amount",
              label: "Enter a custom MON amount",
              type: "number",
            },
          ],
        },
      ],
    },
  };


  // Return the response with proper headers
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(response), {
    status: 200,
    headers,
  });
};

```

### Goldsky Mirror Pipeline Configuration (YAML)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/best-practices

Example `pipeline.yaml` file for configuring Goldsky Mirror to stream Monad testnet ERC20 transfers. It specifies data sources, filtering, transformations using SQL, and a PostgreSQL sink.

```yaml
name: monad-testnet-erc20-transfers
apiVersion: 3
sources:
  monad_testnet_erc20_transfers:
    dataset_name: monad_testnet.erc20_transfers
    filter: address = '0x0' # Add erc20 contract address. Multiple addresses can be added with 'OR' operator: address = '0x0' OR address = '0x1'
    version: 1.2.0
    type: dataset
    start_at: earliest


# Data transformation logic (optional)
transforms:
  select_relevant_fields:
    sql: |
      SELECT
          id,
          address,
          event_signature,
          event_params,
          raw_log.block_number as block_number,
          raw_log.block_hash as block_hash,
          raw_log.transaction_hash as transaction_hash
      FROM
          ethereum_decoded_logs
    primary_key: id


# Sink configuration to specify where data goes eg. DB
sinks:
  postgres:
    type: postgres
    table: erc20_transfers
    schema: goldsky
    secret_name: A_POSTGRESQL_SECRET
    from: select_relevant_fields

```

### Get Delegators

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves a paginated list of delegators for a given validator.

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/monad_xyz/getDelegators

### Description
Retrieves a paginated list of delegators for a given validator, starting from a specified delegator address.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/monad_xyz/getDelegators

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **validatorId** (uint64) - Required - The ID of the validator for which we want to know the delegators.
- **startDelegator** (address) - Required - The address of the delegator to start the pagination from.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **isDone** (bool) - Indicates whether the end of the list has been reached.
- **nextDelegator** (address) - The next delegator address to use for pagination.
- **delegators** (address[]) - An array of delegator addresses.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "isDone": false,
  "nextDelegator": "0xdef456",
  "delegators": [
    "0xabc123",
    "0xdef456",
    "0xghi789"
  ]
}
```
```

### Staking ABI - getExecutionValidatorSet

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves a paginated list of validator IDs in the execution set. Requires a starting index.

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/monad_xyz/getExecutionValidatorSet

### Description
Retrieves a paginated list of validator IDs in the execution set. Requires a starting index.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/monad_xyz/getExecutionValidatorSet

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
- **startIndex** (uint32) - Required - The index to start retrieving validator IDs from.

#### Request Body
None

### Request Example
```
GET /websites/monad_xyz/getExecutionValidatorSet?startIndex=0
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **isDone** (bool) - Indicates if this is the last page of results.
- **nextIndex** (uint32) - The index for the next page of results.
- **valIds** (uint64[]) - An array of validator IDs.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "isDone": false,
  "nextIndex": 100,
  "valIds": [201, 202, 203, 204, 205]
}
```
```

### Monad EVM Transaction Header Start Event Structure (C)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/overview

Defines the C structure for the 'start of transaction header' event payload. This event is recorded after a new transaction is decoded by the EVM and contains key transaction information. The structure includes the transaction hash, sender address, and the nested transaction header.

```c
/// First event recorded when transaction processing starts
struct monad_exec_txn_header_start {
    monad_c_bytes32 txn_hash;     ///< Keccak hash of transaction RLP
    monad_c_address sender;       ///< Recovered sender address
    struct monad_c_eth_txn_header
        txn_header;               ///< Transaction header
};

```

### Get Validator Information

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves detailed information about a specific validator.

```APIDOC
## GET /staking/getValidator

### Description
Retrieves detailed information about a specific validator.

### Method
`view`

### Endpoint
`getValidator`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
- **validatorId** (uint64) - Required - The ID of the validator to retrieve information for.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "validatorId": 123
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **authAddress** (address) - The authorization address of the validator.
- **flags** (uint64) - Flags indicating the validator's status.
- **stake** (uint256) - The total stake amount for the validator.
- **accRewardPerToken** (uint256) - Accumulated reward per token.
- **commission** (uint256) - The current commission rate.
- **unclaimedRewards** (uint256) - The amount of unclaimed rewards.
- **consensusStake** (uint256) - The stake amount recognized by consensus.
- **consensusCommission** (uint256) - The commission amount recognized by consensus.
- **snapshotStake** (uint256) - The stake amount at the last snapshot.
- **snapshotCommission** (uint256) - The commission amount at the last snapshot.
- **secpPubkey** (bytes memory) - The validator's secp public key.
- **blsPubkey** (bytes memory) - The validator's BLS public key.
```

### GET /api/actions/donate-mon

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/blinks-guide

Retrieves Blink metadata and UI configuration for the donation action. This includes how the action appears, required parameters, and execution details.

```APIDOC
## GET /api/actions/donate-mon

### Description
Returns the Blink metadata and UI configuration, detailing how the action appears in Blink clients, user-provided parameters, and execution logic.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/api/actions/donate-mon

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **amount** (string) - Optional - The amount of MON to donate. Can be a pre-defined value or a custom amount specified by the user.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **type** (string) - Indicates the type of action ('action').
- **icon** (string) - URL of the icon for the action.
- **label** (string) - The label displayed for the action (e.g., '1 MON').
- **title** (string) - The title of the action.
- **description** (string) - A detailed description of the action, including links to documentation.
- **links** (object) - Contains links to related actions.
  - **actions** (array) - An array of action links.
    - **type** (string) - The type of the linked action ('transaction').
    - **label** (string) - The label for the linked action.
    - **href** (string) - The endpoint for the linked action.
    - **parameters** (array) - Optional parameters for the action, such as a custom amount input.
      - **name** (string) - The name of the parameter.
      - **label** (string) - The label for the parameter.
      - **type** (string) - The data type of the parameter ('number').

### Response Example
```json
{
  "type": "action",
  "icon": "https://your-domain.com/donate-mon.png",
  "label": "1 MON",
  "title": "Donate MON",
  "description": "This Blink demonstrates how to donate MON on the Monad blockchain. It is a part of the official Blink Starter Guides by Dialect Labs.  \n\nLearn how to build this Blink: https://dialect.to/docs/guides/donate-mon",
  "links": {
    "actions": [
      {
        "type": "transaction",
        "label": "0.01 MON",
        "href": "/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.01"
      },
      {
        "type": "transaction",
        "label": "0.05 MON",
        "href": "/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.05"
      },
      {
        "type": "transaction",
        "label": "0.1 MON",
        "href": "/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.1"
      },
      {
        "type": "transaction",
        "href": "/api/actions/donate-mon?amount={amount}",
        "label": "Donate",
        "parameters": [
          {
            "name": "amount",
            "label": "Enter a custom MON amount",
            "type": "number"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```
```

### Get Validator Sets

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves paginated lists of validator IDs for consensus, snapshot, or execution contexts.

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/monad_xyz/get*ValidatorSet

### Description
These functions return the consensus, snapshot, and execution validator ids, respectively. Each call retrieves up to `PAGINATED_RESULTS_SIZE` validator ids starting from `startIndex` and returns a tuple `(bool done, uint32 nextIndex, uint64[] valids)`.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/monad_xyz/getConsensusValidatorSet
/websites/monad_xyz/getSnapshotValidatorSet
/websites/monad_xyz/getExecutionValidatorSet

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **startIndex** (uint32) - Required - Since the list being looked up is potentially very long, each of these functions is paginated, returning a fixed-length subset of the desired list. Pass `startIndex` to indicate where in the list to start.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **isDone** (bool) - Indicates whether the end of the list has been reached.
- **nextIndex** (uint32) - The index after the last slot in the array.
- **valIds** (uint64[]) - An array of validator IDs.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "isDone": false,
  "nextIndex": 100,
  "valIds": [
    101,
    102,
    103
  ]
}
```
```

### Staking ABI - getDelegations

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves a paginated list of validator IDs a delegator has staked with. Requires delegator address and starting validator ID.

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/monad_xyz/getDelegations

### Description
Retrieves a paginated list of validator IDs a delegator has staked with. Requires delegator address and starting validator ID.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/monad_xyz/getDelegations

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
- **delegator** (address) - Required - The address of the delegator.
- **startValId** (uint64) - Required - The validator ID to start retrieving delegations from.

#### Request Body
None

### Request Example
```
GET /websites/monad_xyz/getDelegations?delegator=0x123...&startValId=0
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **isDone** (bool) - Indicates if this is the last page of results.
- **nextValId** (uint64) - The validator ID for the next page of results.
- **valIds** (uint64[]) - An array of validator IDs the delegator has staked with.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "isDone": false,
  "nextValId": 200,
  "valIds": [101, 105, 110]
}
```
```

### Get Delegations

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves a paginated list of validator IDs to which a given delegator has delegated.

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/monad_xyz/getDelegations

### Description
Each call retrieves up to `PAGINATED_RESULTS_SIZE` validator ids starting from `startValId` and returns a tuple `(bool isDone, uint64 nextValId, uint64[] valIds)` with delegation from the input `delegator` address. The `bool isdone` indicates whether the end of the list was reached. The `uint64 nextValId` is the id after the last element in `valIds`. Use it as the `startValId` for the next call. To capture the full set, make the first function call using `startValId = 0`.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/monad_xyz/getDelegations

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **delegator** (address) - Required - The address whose delegations we want to look up.
- **startValId** (uint64) - Required - The starting validator ID for pagination.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **isDone** (bool) - Indicates whether the end of the list has been reached.
- **nextValId** (uint64) - The ID after the last element in `valIds`.
- **valIds** (uint64[]) - An array of validator IDs the delegator has delegated to.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "isDone": false,
  "nextValId": 150,
  "valIds": [
    101,
    102,
    103
  ]
}
```
```

### Get Validator Information

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/monad_xyz/getValidator

### Description
This is the primary method to obtain information about a validator state. It provides a complete view of the validator’s state across execution, consensus, and snapshot contexts.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/monad_xyz/getValidator

### Parameters

### Response

- **flags** (uint64) - Flags associated with the validator.
- **stake** (uint256) - The stake amount of the validator.
- **accRewardPerToken** (uint256) - Accumulated reward per token.
- **commission** (uint256) - The commission rate.
- **unclaimedRewards** (uint256) - The amount of unclaimed rewards.
- **consensusStake** (uint256) - Stake in the consensus context.
- **consensusCommission** (uint256) - Commission in the consensus context.
- **snapshotStake** (uint256) - Stake in the snapshot context.
- **snapshotCommission** (uint256) - Commission in the snapshot context.
- **secpPubkey** (bytes memory) - The secp public key.
- **blsPubkey** (bytes memory) - The BLS public key.

#### Response Example

  "authAddress": "0xabc123",
  "flags": 1,
  "stake": "1000000000000000000",
  "accRewardPerToken": "50000000000000000",
  "commission": "10000000000000000",
  "unclaimedRewards": "20000000000000000",
  "consensusStake": "1000000000000000000",
  "consensusCommission": "10000000000000000",
  "snapshotStake": "950000000000000000",
  "snapshotCommission": "10000000000000000",

}
```

### Counter Smart Contract (Solidity)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/deploy-smart-contract/foundry

A simple Solidity smart contract named `Counter` that includes a state variable `number` and functions to set and increment it. This contract is a basic example for deployment.

```solidity
1234567891011121314
// SPDX-License-Identifier: UNLICENSED
pragma solidity ^0.8.13;


contract Counter {
    uint256 public number;


    function setNumber(uint256 newNumber) public {
        number = newNumber;
    }


    function increment() public {
        number++;
    }
}

```

* **`apple-touch-icon.png`**- iOS home screen icon (180×180px)

### CatToken Solidity Test Suite

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/indexers/ghost

Provides a test suite for the CatToken contract using Foundry. It includes setup for the contract, owner, and user addresses, and tests the initial supply and token transfer functionality.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

import "forge-std/Test.sol";
import "../src/CatToken.sol";

contract CatTokenTest is Test {
    CatToken public token;
    address public owner;
    address public user;

    function setUp() public {
        owner = address(this);
        user = address(0x1);

        token = new CatToken();
    }

    function testInitialSupply() public view {
        assertEq(token.totalSupply(), 1_000_000_000 * 10**18);
        assertEq(token.balanceOf(owner), 1_000_000_000 * 10**18);
    }

    function testTransfer() public {
        uint256 amount = 1_000_000 * 10**18;
        token.transfer(user, amount);
        assertEq(token.balanceOf(user), amount);
        assertEq(token.balanceOf(owner), 999_000_000 * 10**18);
    }
}
```

/api/actions/donate-mon

### Query Parameters
- **amount** (number) - Optional - The amount of MON to donate. If not provided, default options might be displayed.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **type** (string) - Specifies the type of action, 'action' for Blink actions.
- **icon** (string) - URL to the icon representing the action.
- **label** (string) - A short label for the action (e.g., '1 MON').
- **title** (string) - The title of the Blink action.
- **description** (string) - A detailed description of the action.
- **links** (object) - Contains links to different actions or transaction types.
  - **actions** (array) - An array of action objects.
    - **type** (string) - Type of the action, 'transaction' for blockchain transactions.
    - **label** (string) - Label for the specific transaction amount (e.g., '0.01 MON').
    - **href** (string) - The endpoint for the transaction POST request, potentially including amount placeholders.
    - **parameters** (array) - Optional. Defines custom input parameters for transactions (e.g., a custom amount).

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "type": "action",
  "icon": "https://your-domain.com/donate-mon.png",
  "label": "1 MON",
  "title": "Donate MON",
  "description": "This Blink demonstrates how to donate MON on the Monad blockchain.",
  "links": {
    "actions": [
      {
        "type": "transaction",
        "label": "0.01 MON",
        "href": "/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.01"
      },
      {
        "type": "transaction",
        "label": "Donate",
        "href": "/api/actions/donate-mon?amount={amount}",
        "parameters": [
          {
            "name": "amount",
            "label": "Enter a custom MON amount",
            "type": "number"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```
```

### Example Reward Calculation

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-behavior

Illustrates how a delegator receives rewards based on their stake percentage, validator commission, and total rewards. This calculation shows the pro rata distribution of rewards after commission is deducted.

```plaintext
If reward for the block is 10 MON, commission is 10%, and you have 20% of the validator's total stake, you would receive:
10 MON * 90% * 20% = 1.8 MON
```

### Handle GET Request for Blink UI Configuration

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/blinks-guide

This endpoint defines the metadata and UI configuration for the 'Donate MON' Blink. It specifies the icon, label, title, description, and available actions, including transaction links with varying amounts and a custom input for donations. It returns a JSON response conforming to the ActionGetResponse interface.

```typescript
import { ActionGetResponse, ActionPostResponse } from "@solana/actions";
import { serialize } from "wagmi";
import { parseEther } from "viem";


// CAIP-2 format for Monad
const blockchain = `eip155:10143`;


// Wallet address that will receive the donations
const donationWallet = `<RECEIVER_ADDRESS>`;


// Create headers with CAIP blockchain ID
const headers = {
  "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
  "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": "GET, POST, OPTIONS",
  "Access-Control-Allow-Headers":
  "Content-Type, x-blockchain-ids, x-action-version",
  "Content-Type": "application/json",
  "x-blockchain-ids": blockchain,
  "x-action-version": "2.0",
};


// OPTIONS endpoint is required for CORS preflight requests
// Your Blink won't render if you don't add this
export const OPTIONS = async () => {
  return new Response(null, { headers });
};


// GET endpoint returns the Blink metadata (JSON) and UI configuration
export const GET = async (req: Request) => {
  // This JSON is used to render the Blink UI
  const response: ActionGetResponse = {
    type: "action",
    icon: `${new URL("/donate-mon.png", req.url).toString()}`,
    label: "1 MON",
    title: "Donate MON",
    description:
      "This Blink demonstrates how to donate MON on the Monad blockchain. It is a part of the official Blink Starter Guides by Dialect Labs.  \n\nLearn how to build this Blink: https://dialect.to/docs/guides/donate-mon",
    // Links is used if you have multiple actions or if you need more than one params
    links: {
      actions: [
        {
          // Defines this as a blockchain transaction
          type: "transaction",
          label: "0.01 MON",
          // This is the endpoint for the POST request
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.01`,
        },
        {
          type: "transaction",
          label: "0.05 MON",
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.05`,
        },
        {
          type: "transaction",
          label: "0.1 MON",
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount=0.1`,
        },
        {
          // Example for a custom input field
          type: "transaction",
          href: `/api/actions/donate-mon?amount={amount}`,
          label: "Donate",
          parameters: [
            {
              name: "amount",
              label: "Enter a custom MON amount",
              type: "number",
            },
          ],
        },
      ],
    },
  };


  // Return the response with proper headers
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(response), {
    status: 200,
    headers,
  });
};


// POST endpoint handles the actual transaction creation
export const POST = async (req: Request) => {
    try {
      // Extract amount from URL
      const url = new URL(req.url);
      const amount = url.searchParams.get("amount");


      if (!amount) {
          throw new Error("Amount is required");
      }


      // Build the transaction
      const transaction = {
          to: donationWallet,
          value: parseEther(amount).toString(),
          chainId: 10143,
      };


      const transactionJson = serialize(transaction);


      // Build ActionPostResponse
      const response: ActionPostResponse = {
          type: "transaction",
          transaction: transactionJson,
          message: "Donate MON",
      };


      // Return the response with proper headers
      return new Response(JSON.stringify(response), {
          status: 200,
          headers,
      });
    } catch (error) {
      // Log and return an error response
      console.error("Error processing request:", error);
      return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "Internal server error" }), {
        status: 500,
        headers,
      });
  }
};

```

### GET /validator/{validatorId}

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves detailed information about a specific validator, including its address, flags, stake, commission, and public keys.

```APIDOC
## GET /validator/{validatorId}

### Description
Retrieves detailed information about a specific validator, including its address, flags, stake, commission, and public keys.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/validator/{validatorId}

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **validatorId** (uint64) - Required - The unique identifier of the validator.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **authAddress** (address) - The authentication address of the validator.
- **flags** (uint64) - Flags associated with the validator's status.
- **stake** (uint256) - The total stake amount for the validator.
- **accRewardPerToken** (uint256) - Accumulated reward per token for the validator.
- **commission** (uint256) - The commission rate set by the validator.
- **unclaimedRewards** (uint256) - The amount of unclaimed rewards for the validator.
- **consensusStake** (uint256) - The stake amount used for consensus.
- **consensusCommission** (uint256) - The commission rate for consensus.
- **snapshotStake** (uint256) - The stake amount at the last snapshot.
- **snapshotCommission** (uint256) - The commission rate at the last snapshot.
- **secpPubkey** (bytes) - The secp256k1 public key of the validator.
- **blsPubkey** (bytes) - The BLS public key of the validator.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "authAddress": "0x1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef12345678",
  "flags": 1,
  "stake": "1000000000000000000",
  "accRewardPerToken": "50000000000000000",
  "commission": "10000000000000000",
  "unclaimedRewards": "10000000000000000",
  "consensusStake": "1000000000000000000",
  "consensusCommission": "10000000000000000",
  "snapshotStake": "950000000000000000",
  "snapshotCommission": "10000000000000000",
  "secpPubkey": "0xabcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef12",
  "blsPubkey": "0x1122334455667788990011223344556677889900112233445566778899001122"
}
```
```

### GET /withdrawal/{validatorId}/{delegator}/{withdrawalId}

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Retrieves information about a specific withdrawal request, including the amount, accumulated rewards, and epoch.

```APIDOC
## GET /withdrawal/{validatorId}/{delegator}/{withdrawalId}

### Description
Retrieves information about a specific withdrawal request, including the amount, accumulated rewards, and epoch.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/withdrawal/{validatorId}/{delegator}/{withdrawalId}

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **validatorId** (uint64) - Required - The unique identifier of the validator.
- **delegator** (address) - Required - The address of the delegator.
- **withdrawalId** (uint8) - Required - The identifier of the withdrawal request.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **withdrawalAmount** (uint256) - The amount requested for withdrawal.
- **accRewardPerToken** (uint256) - Accumulated reward per token at the time of request.
- **withdrawEpoch** (uint64) - The epoch in which the withdrawal is scheduled.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "withdrawalAmount": "500000000000000000",
  "accRewardPerToken": "60000000000000000",
  "withdrawEpoch": 100
}
```
```

export default function MyComponent() {
  const { haptics } = useFrame();

  return (
    <div>

    </div>
  );
}

### Python: Subscribe to New Block Headers

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/reference/websockets

Demonstrates subscribing to new block header events using a Python library that closely matches the Geth real-time events protocol. It shows explicit calls to subscribe with a specific subscription name ('newHeads').

```python
import websocket
import json

ws = websocket.create_connection("ws://localhost:8546")

request = {
    "jsonrpc": "2.0",
    "method": "eth_subscribe",
    "params": ["newHeads"],
    "id": 1
}

ws.send(json.dumps(request))

while True:
    result = ws.recv()
    print(json.loads(result))
```

### Envio HyperIndex Configuration (config.yaml)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/best-practices

Configuration file for Envio HyperIndex to index Monad Testnet data. Specifies network ID, optional RPC configuration, and contract event handlers.

```yaml
name: your-indexers-name
networks:
- id: 10143  # Monad Testnet
  # Optional custom RPC configuration - only add if default indexing has issues
  # rpc_config:
  #   url: YOUR_RPC_URL_HERE  # Replace with your RPC URL (e.g., from Alchemy)
  #   interval_ceiling: 50     # Maximum number of blocks to fetch in a single request
  #   acceleration_additive: 10  # Speed up factor for block fetching
  #   initial_block_interval: 10  # Initial block fetch interval size
  start_block: 0  # Replace with the block you want to start indexing from
  contracts:
  - name: YourContract  # Replace with your contract name
    address:
    - 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000  # Replace with your contract address
    # Add more addresses if needed for multiple deployments of the same contract
    handler: src/EventHandlers.ts
    events:
    # Replace with your event signatures
    # Format: EventName(paramType paramName, paramType2 paramName2, ...)
    # Example: Transfer(address from, address to, uint256 amount)
    # Example: OrderCreated(uint40 orderId, address owner, uint96 size, uint32 price, bool isBuy)
    - event: EventOne(paramType1 paramName1, paramType2 paramName2)
    # Add more events as needed

```

### Monad Event Descriptor Structure (C)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/event-ring

Illustrates a C structure `monad_event_descriptor` that contains an array `content_ext` used for storing flow tags. The example shows how to access transaction IDs and account indices from this array.

```c
struct monad_event_descriptor event;
// ... initialization code ...
// Accessing transaction ID (index + 1, or 0 if no transaction)
int transaction_id = event.content_ext[MONAD_FLOW_TXN_ID];
// Accessing account index
int account_index = event.content_ext[MONAD_FLOW_ACCOUNT_INDEX];

```

### Optimized EVM Bytecode - Constant Folding Result

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/monad-arch/execution/native-compilation

The result of applying constant folding to the previous EVM bytecode example. The 'PUSH1 0x2', 'PUSH1 0x3', and 'ADD' sequence is replaced by a single 'PUSH1 0x5', while maintaining the same gas cost and semantic behavior.

```evm
PUSH1 0x5
```

### Configure Farcaster Mini App Details (JavaScript)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/templates/farcaster-miniapp/publishing-miniapp

This JavaScript snippet shows the structure of the `farcaster.json` configuration object. It includes essential details like the app's name, icon, URLs, and webhook. The `accountAssociation` is a placeholder for publisher verification.

```javascript
const appUrl = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_URL;
const farcasterConfig = {
    // accountAssociation details are required to associate the published app with it's author
    // instructions on how to get these values are provided later in this guide.
    accountAssociation: {
        "header": "",
        "payload": "",
        "signature": ""
    },

        version: "1",
        name: "Monad Farcaster Mini App Template", // Name of your Mini App
        iconUrl: `${appUrl}/images/icon.png`, // Icon of the app in the app store
        homeUrl: `${appUrl}`, // Default launch URL
        imageUrl: `${appUrl}/images/feed.png`, // Default image to show if shared in a feed.
        screenshotUrls: [], // Visual previews of the app
        tags: ["monad", "farcaster", "miniapp", "template"], // Descriptive tags for search
        primaryCategory: "developer-tools",
        buttonTitle: "Launch Template",
        splashImageUrl: `${appUrl}/images/splash.png`, // URL of image to show on loading screen.	
        splashBackgroundColor: "#ffffff", // Hex color code to use on loading screen.

### EVM Bytecode Fragment - Constant Folding

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/monad-arch/execution/native-compilation

An example of EVM bytecode where two numbers are pushed onto the stack and then added. A compiler can perform constant folding by pre-calculating the result of the ADD operation, simplifying the code.

```evm
PUSH1 0x2
PUSH1 0x3
ADD
```

### Solidity Script for Token Transfers

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/indexers/ghost

This Solidity script, written for Foundry, allows for the transfer of CatTokens to specified addresses on the Monad testnet. It requires private key and token address to be set as environment variables. The script broadcasts the transactions after starting the broadcast with a private key.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.19;


import "forge-std/Script.sol";
import "../src/CatToken.sol";


contract TransferCatTokens is Script {
    function run() external {
        uint256 deployerPrivateKey = vm.envUint("PRIVATE_KEY");
        address token = vm.envAddress("TOKEN_ADDRESS");


        vm.startBroadcast(deployerPrivateKey);


        // Send tokens to test addresses
        CatToken(token).transfer(address(0x1), 1000 * 10**18);
        CatToken(token).transfer(address(0x2), 2000 * 10**18);
        CatToken(token).transfer(address(0x3), 3000 * 10**18);


        vm.stopBroadcast();
    }
}

```

### JavaScript WebSocket: Subscribe to New Blocks

```javascript
import { WebSocketProvider } from "ethers/providers";


const wsUrl = "wss://testnet-rpc.monad.xyz"


const provider = new WebSocketProvider(wsUrl);


provider.on("block", (blockNumber) => {
  console.log("New block received:", blockNumber);
});

```

### Create Context Providers

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/blinks-guide

Defines React context providers for Wagmi, React Query, and ConnectKit to manage application state and wallet connections. Requires 'use client' directive in Next.js.

```typescript
"use client";


import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { ConnectKitProvider } from "connectkit";
import { type PropsWithChildren } from "react";
import { WagmiProvider } from "wagmi";
import { config } from "@/config";


const queryClient = new QueryClient();


export const Providers = ({ children }: PropsWithChildren) => {
  return (
    <WagmiProvider config={config}>
      <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
        <ConnectKitProvider>{children}</ConnectKitProvider>
      </QueryClientProvider>
    </WagmiProvider>
  );
};

```

### Create Image Generation API Route (Next.js 14+)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/templates/farcaster-miniapp/generating-custom-og-images

This TypeScript code defines the `app/api/og/route.tsx` file for Next.js 14+. It sets the route to be dynamically generated and handles GET requests to create OG images based on query parameters.

```typescript
import { ImageResponse } from '@vercel/og';
import { NextRequest } from 'next/server';


// Generate the image for every request
export const dynamic = "force-dynamic";


export async function GET(request: NextRequest) {
  try {
    const { searchParams } = new URL(request.url);
    
    // The below is dependent on whether the username and image are passed as query params or not.
    const username = searchParams.get('username') || 'User'; // Username of the user
    const imageUrl = searchParams.get('image') || ''; // Image url of the user
    
    return new ImageResponse(
    (
        // Generate the image here
    );
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('Error generating OG image:', e);
    return new Response('Failed to generate image', { status: 500 });
  }
}
```

### Update API Routes with Slippage Parameter (TypeScript)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/templates/next-serwist-0x-privy-embedded-wallet

Modifies the GET request handler in the API routes to accept and include a 'slippageBps' parameter. It ensures a default slippage value is set if none is provided in the search parameters.

```typescript
export async function GET(request: NextRequest) {
  const searchParams = request.nextUrl.searchParams;

  // Add default slippage if not provided
  if (!searchParams.has("slippageBps")) {
    searchParams.set("slippageBps", "100"); // 1% default
  }

  const res = await fetch(
    `https://api.0x.org/swap/permit2/price?${searchParams}`, // or /quote
    {
      headers: {
        "0x-api-key": process.env.ZEROX_API_KEY as string,
        "0x-version": "v2",
      },
    }
  );
  const data = await res.json();
  return Response.json(data);
}
```

### Python WebSocket: Subscribe to New Heads

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/reference/websockets

Connects to Monad's WebSocket RPC endpoint using web3.py and subscribes to the 'newHeads' event to receive notifications for new block headers. It then continuously prints the block number of each received block.

```python
import asyncio


from web3 import AsyncWeb3
from web3.providers.persistent import WebSocketProvider


ws_url = 'wss://testnet-rpc.monad.xyz'


async def print_latest():
  async with AsyncWeb3(WebSocketProvider(ws_url)) as w3:
    subscription_id = await w3.eth.subscribe('newHeads', {{}})
    async for payload in w3.socket.process_subscriptions():
      print(f"New block received: {payload['result']['number']}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
  asyncio.run(print_latest())

```

### Handle Deep Links in Expo App (_layout.tsx)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/deeplinks-using-expo

Implement a `DeepLinkHandler` component in your Expo app's entry point to manage initial deep links and listen for subsequent URL changes. This example uses `Linking.parse` to extract information and `useRouter` for navigation.

```tsx
import { Linking } from 'react-native';
import { useRouter } from 'expo-router';
import React, { useEffect } from 'react';

interface SwapDeeplinkParams {
  from?: string;
  to?: string;
  amount?: string;
}

// Function to parse the deep link and get the hostname and queryParams
function parseSwapDeeplink(url: string): SwapDeeplinkParams | null {
  try {
    const { hostname, queryParams } = Linking.parse(url);
    
    if (hostname !== 'swap' || !queryParams) {
      return null;
    }

    return {
      from: queryParams.from as string | undefined,
      to: queryParams.to as string | undefined,
      amount: queryParams.amount as string | undefined,
    };
  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Error parsing deeplink:', error);
    return null;
  }
}

function DeeplinkHandler({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  const router = useRouter();
  
  useEffect(() => {
    
    const handleDeeplink = (url: string) => {
      // Parse the deep link and get the params (host, path, params etc...)
      const params = parseSwapDeeplink(url);
      if (params) {
        // The example here makes the params globally accessible in the app, however you can use React Context or similar to make the params accessible from anywhere in the app.
        (global as any).swapDeeplinkParams = params;
        // Based on the path or host you can redirect the user to the respective screen in the app 
        router.replace('/');
      }
    };

    // Handle initial URL
    Linking.getInitialURL().then(url => url && handleDeeplink(url));

    // Create an event listener and handle URL changes while app is open
    const subscription = Linking.addEventListener('url', event => handleDeeplink(event.url));

    // Removes the event listener when the component is destroyed (avoids memory leaks)
    return () => subscription.remove();
  }, [router]);

  return <>{children}</>;
}

export default function Layout() {
    // ... other layout code

    return (
        <DeeplinkHandler>
            {/* Assume App component is defined elsewhere */}
            {/* <App /> */}
        </DeeplinkHandler>
    );
 }
```

### Implement MON Balance Check Tool

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/monad-mcp

Implements the 'get-mon-balance' tool by adding logic to fetch the MON balance for a given address on Monad testnet using Viem's publicClient. Includes error handling.

```typescript
server.tool(
    // Tool ID 
    "get-mon-balance",
    // Description of what the tool does
    "Get MON balance for an address on Monad testnet",
    // Input schema
    {
        address: z.string().describe("Monad testnet address to check balance for"),
    },
    // Tool implementation
    async ({ address }) => {
        try {
            // Check MON balance for the input address
            const balance = await publicClient.getBalance({
                address: address as `0x${string}`,
            });


            // Return a human friendly message indicating the balance.
            return {
                content: [
                    {
                        type: "text",
                        text: `Balance for ${address}: ${formatUnits(balance, 18)} MON`,
                    },
                ],
            };
        } catch (error) {
            // If the balance check process fails, return a graceful message back to the MCP client indicating a failure.
            return {
                content: [
                    {
                        type: "text",
                        text: `Failed to retrieve balance for address: ${address}. Error: ${
                        error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)
                        }`,
                    },
                ],
            };
        }
    }
);
```

### Indexer Head Check and Wei to Ether Conversion

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/indexers/tg-bot-using-envio

This TypeScript file provides utility functions for the HyperIndex event handler. `isIndexingAtHead` checks if the current event's timestamp is greater than or equal to the indexer's start time, ensuring notifications are sent only for recent events. `weiToEth` converts a BigInt value representing Wei to a human-readable Ether string, handling decimal places.

```typescript
// Used to ensure notifications are only sent while indexing at the head and not historical sync
const INDEXER_START_TIMESTAMP = Math.floor(new Date().getTime() / 1000);


export const isIndexingAtHead = (timestamp: number): boolean => {
    return timestamp >= INDEXER_START_TIMESTAMP;
}


// Convert wei to ether for human readability
export const weiToEth = (bigIntNumber: bigint): string => {
  // Convert BigInt to string
  const numberString = bigIntNumber.toString();


  const decimalPointsInEth = 18;


  // Extract integer part and decimal part
  const integerPart = numberString.substring(
    0,
    numberString.length - decimalPointsInEth
  );


  const decimalPart = numberString.slice(-decimalPointsInEth);


  // Insert decimal point
  const decimalString = 
    (integerPart ? integerPart : "0") +
    "." +
    decimalPart.padStart(decimalPointsInEth, "0");


  // Add negative sign if necessary
  return decimalString.slice(0, -14);
};

```

### Submit Transactions Concurrently (JavaScript)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/best-practices

```javascript
1234567891011
for (let i = 0; i < TIMES; i++) {

    account: ACCOUNT,

```

```javascript
12345678910111213

  .fill(null)
  .map(async (_, i) => {

const results = await Promise.all(resultPromises)

### Implement Blink Component and Hooks

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/blinks-guide

Renders a Blink component for interacting with a specific blockchain action, utilizing Wagmi and ConnectKit for wallet integration. Includes necessary imports and hooks for managing the blink and wallet connection.

```typescript
"use client";


import {
  Blink,
  useBlink,
  useActionsRegistryInterval,
} from "@dialectlabs/blinks";


import "@dialectlabs/blinks/index.css";


import { useEvmWagmiAdapter } from "@dialectlabs/blinks/hooks/evm";


import { ConnectKitButton, useModal } from "connectkit";


export default function Home() {
  // Actions registry interval
  useActionsRegistryInterval();


  // ConnectKit modal
  const { setOpen } = useModal();


  // Wagmi adapter, used to connect to the wallet
  const { adapter } = useEvmWagmiAdapter({
    onConnectWalletRequest: async () => {
      setOpen(true);
    },
  });


  // Action we want to execute in the Blink
  const { blink, isLoading } = useBlink({
    url: "evm-action:http://localhost:3000/api/actions/donate-mon",
  });


  return (
    <main className="flex flex-col items-center justify-center">
      <ConnectKitButton />
      <div className="w-1/2 lg:px-4 lg:p-8">
        {isLoading || !blink ? (
          <span>Loading</span>
        ) : (
          // Blink component, used to execute the action
          <Blink blink={blink} adapter={adapter} securityLevel="all" />
        )}
      </div>
    </main>
  );
}

```

### Subgraph Configuration (subgraph.yaml)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/best-practices

Defines the subgraph's data sources, including the network, contract address, ABI, and event handlers. It specifies the mapping file and language (wasm/assemblyscript) for event processing.

```yaml
specVersion: 1.2.0
indexerHints:
  prune: auto
schema:
  file: ./schema.graphql
dataSources:
  - kind: ethereum
    name: YourContractName # Replace with your contract name
    network: monad-testnet # Monad testnet configuration
    source:
      address: "0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" # Replace with your contract address
      abi: YourContractABI # Replace with your contract ABI name
      startBlock: 0 # Replace with the block where your contract was deployed/where you want to index from
    mapping:
      kind: ethereum/events
      apiVersion: 0.0.9
      language: wasm/assemblyscript
      entities:
        # List your entities here - these should match those defined in schema.graphql
        # - Entity1
        # - Entity2
      abis:
        - name: YourContractABI # Should match the ABI name specified above
          file: ./abis/YourContract.json # Path to your contract ABI JSON file
      eventHandlers:
        # Add your event handlers here, for example:
        # - event: EventName(param1Type, param2Type, ...)
        #   handler: handleEventName
      file: ./src/mapping.ts # Path to your event handler implementations

```

### Monad Gas Cost Formula for CREATE and CREATE2 Opcodes

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/opcode-pricing

This code snippet illustrates the calculation of gas costs for the CREATE and CREATE2 opcodes in Monad. It incorporates factors like minimum word size, initialization code cost, hashing cost (specific to CREATE2), and the significantly increased code deposit cost per byte.

```python
minimum_word_size = (size + 31) / 32
init_code_cost = 2 * minimum_word_size
hash_cost = 6 * minimum_word_size if is_create2 else 0  # only for CREATE2
code_deposit_cost = 1200 * deployed_code_size  # raised from 200

static_gas = 160000  # raised from 32000
dynamic_gas = init_code_cost + hash_cost + memory_expansion_cost + \
    deployment_code_execution_cost + code_deposit_cost
```

--------------------------------

### Pseudocode for Validator Creation and Stake Management

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

This pseudocode outlines the steps involved in creating a new validator, including validating public keys, managing stake amounts, and setting validator flags based on stake thresholds. It also details how delegation affects stake epochs and accumulator counts.

```pseudocode
secp_pubkey, bls_pubkey, auth_address, amount, commission = payload


assert amount == msg.value


// increment validator id
last_val_id = last_val_id + 1;


// set uniqueness of keys
secp_to_val_id[secp_eth_address] = last_val_id;
bls_to_val_id[bls_eth_address] = last_val_id;


// set validator info
val_execution[last_val_id] = ValExecution{
    uint256 stake = msg.value;
    uint256 commission = commission;
    KeysPacked keys = KeysPacked{secp_pubkey, bls_pubkey}
    uint256 address_flags = set_flags();
}


// set authority delegator info
delegator[last_val_id][input.auth_address] = DelInfo{
    uint256 delta_stake = set_stake()[0];
    uint256 next_delta_stake = set_stake()[1];
    uint64 delta_epoch = set_stake()[2];
    uint64 next_delta_epoch = set_stake()[3];
}


// set delegator accumulator
epoch_acc[last_val_id][getEpoch()] = Accumulator{
    uint256 ref_count += 1;
}


// set flags
set_flags();


// push validator id
if (val_execution[last_val_id].stake() >= ACTIVE_VALIDATOR_STAKE
        and last_val_id not in execution_valset):
    execution_valset.push(last_val_id);


return last_val_id;


def set_flags():
    if msg.value + val_execution[last_val_id].stake() >= ACTIVE_VALIDATOR_STAKE:
        return ValidatorFlagsOk;
    if msg.value + val_execution[last_val_id].stake() >= MIN_VALIDATE_STAKE
        return ValidatorFlagsStakeTooLow;

def set_stake():
    if in_boundary:
        delta_stake = 0;
        next_delta_stake = msg.value;
        delta_epoch = 0;
        next_delta_epoch = current_epoch + 2;
    else:
        delta_stake = msg.value;
        next_delta_stake = 0;
        delta_epoch = current_epoch + 1;
        next_delta_epoch = 0;
    return [delta_stake, next_delta_stake, delta_epoch, next_delta_epoch];
```

### Solidity Smart Contract for Greeting

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/deploy-smart-contract/remix

This Solidity code defines a simple smart contract named Gmonad with a greeting message. It includes a constructor to initialize the greeting and a function to set a new greeting. The pragma statement specifies the compatible Solidity compiler versions.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT


// Make sure the compiler version is below 0.8.24 since Cancun compiler is not supported just yet
pragma solidity >=0.8.0 <=0.8.24;


contract Gmonad {
    string public greeting;


    constructor(string memory _greeting) {
        greeting = _greeting;
    }


    function setGreeting(string calldata _greeting) external {
        greeting = _greeting;
    }
}
```

### RPC Differences Overview

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/reference/rpc-differences

General differences in RPC behavior between Monad and Geth.

```APIDOC
## General RPC Behavior Differences

### Description
Monad aims to align with Geth's RPC behavior but has key differences due to architectural variations.

### Method
N/A (Overview)

### Endpoint
N/A (Overview)

### Parameters
N/A (Overview)

### Request Example
N/A (Overview)

### Response
N/A (Overview)

## Specific RPC Method Differences

### `eth_getLogs`

*   **Description**: Currently has a maximum block range of 100 blocks. It is recommended to use small block ranges (1-10 blocks) for optimal performance. Exceeding this limit may result in long fulfillment times and timeouts.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)
*   **Parameters**:
    *   **Query Parameters**:
        *   `blockRange` (integer) - Optional - Maximum number of blocks to query (default/max is 100).

### `eth_sendRawTransaction`

*   **Description**: May not immediately reject transactions with nonce gaps or insufficient gas. Asynchronous execution means the RPC server might not have the latest world view, allowing these transactions as they might become valid during block creation.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)

### `eth_call` and `eth_estimateGas`

*   **Description**: Do not accept EIP-4844 transaction types as EIP-4844 is not supported.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)

### `eth_maxPriorityFeePerGas`

*   **Description**: Currently returns a hardcoded suggested fee of 2 gwei. This is a temporary measure.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)

### `eth_feeHistory`

*   **Description**: Currently returns default values. This is also a temporary measure.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)

## WebSocket (`eth_subscribe`) Behavior Differences

### Unsupported Subscription Types

*   **Description**: `eth_subscribe` does not support the `syncing` or `newPendingTransactions` subscription types.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)

### Reorganizations in Subscriptions

*   **Description**: Reorganizations never occur in `newHeads` and `logs` subscription types because only real-time data for **finalized** blocks is presented.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)

### Monad-Specific Extensions

*   **Description**: Monad provides extensions to `newHeads` and `logs` subscription types, named `monadNewHeads` and `monadLogs`, to offer better latency.
*   **Method**: POST
*   **Endpoint**: `/` (or relevant RPC endpoint)
*   **Parameters**:
    *   **Query Parameters**:
        *   `subscriptionType` (string) - Optional - Can be `monadNewHeads` or `monadLogs` for enhanced latency.

```

### eth_getCode

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/reference/json-rpc/eth_getCode

Retrieves the code at a given address on the Monad testnet.

```APIDOC
## eth_getCode

### Description
Returns code at a given address.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
https://testnet-rpc.monad.xyz

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **id** (number) - Optional - The ID of the request.
- **jsonrpc** (string) - Required - The JSON-RPC version, must be "2.0".
- **method** (string) - Required - The method name, must be "eth_getCode".
- **params** (array) - Required - An array containing the parameters for the method.
  - **account** (string) - Required - The address to get the code from.
  - **block** (oneOf: BlockTags (oneOf: Quantity (hex) | string) | FixedData_for_32 (string)) - Required - The block at which to get the code.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "id": 0,
  "jsonrpc": "2.0",
  "method": "eth_getCode",
  "params": [
    "0xADDRESS",
    "latest"
  ]
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **result** (string) - The code at the specified address.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "id": 0,
  "jsonrpc": "2.0",
  "result": "0xCODE"
}
```
```

### Monad BLOCK_START Event Structure

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/consensus-events

Details the 'monad_exec_block_start' event structure, recorded at the beginning of block execution. It includes block tag, round, epoch, parent hash, chain ID, and execution inputs.

```c
#include <stdint.h>

// Assuming monad_c_bytes32 and monad_c_uint256_ne are defined as in the block tag example
typedef unsigned char monad_c_bytes32[32];
typedef unsigned char monad_c_uint256_ne[32];

// Forward declaration for monad_c_eth_block_exec_input
struct monad_c_eth_block_exec_input;

// Assuming monad_exec_block_tag is defined as above
struct monad_exec_block_tag;

/// Event recorded at the start of block execution
struct monad_exec_block_start
{
    struct monad_exec_block_tag
        block_tag;                      ///< Execution is for this block
    uint64_t block_round;               ///< Round when block was proposed
    uint64_t epoch;                     ///< Epoch when block was proposed
    monad_c_bytes32 parent_eth_hash;    ///< Hash of Ethereum parent block
    monad_c_uint256_ne chain_id;        ///< Block chain we're associated with
    struct monad_c_eth_block_exec_input
        exec_input;                     ///< Ethereum execution inputs
};

```

### Verify Contract using Foundry (Monad Template)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/verify-smart-contract/foundry

This command verifies a smart contract on the Monad network using the 'sourcify' verifier. It requires the contract address, contract name, Monad chain ID (10143), and the Sourcify API URL. This is the recommended method if using the `foundry-monad` template.

```bash
forge verify-contract \
    <contract_address> \
    <contract_name> \
    --chain 10143 \
    --verifier sourcify \
    --verifier-url https://sourcify-api-monad.blockvision.org
```

### Event Handling Logic (mappings.ts)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/best-practices

Contains the AssemblyScript code for handling smart contract events. It maps event parameters to defined entities and saves them to the store, including metadata like block number and transaction hash.

```typescript
import {
  // Import your contract events here
  // Format: EventName as EventNameEvent
  EventOne as EventOneEvent,
  // Add more events as needed
} from "../generated/YourContractName/YourContractABI" // Replace with your contract name, abi name you supplied in subgraph.yaml


import {
  // Import your schema entities here
  // These should match the entities defined in schema.graphql
  EventOne,
  // Add more entities as needed
} from "../generated/schema"


/**
  * Handler for EventOne
  * Update the function parameters and body according to your event structure
  */
export function handleEventOne(event: EventOneEvent): void {
  // Create a unique ID for this entity
  let entity = new EventOne(
    event.transaction.hash.concatI32(event.logIndex.toI32())
  )
  
  // Map event parameters to entity fields
  // entity.paramName = event.params.paramName
  
  // Example:
  // entity.sender = event.params.sender
  // entity.amount = event.params.amount


  // Add metadata fields
  entity.blockNumber = event.block.number
  entity.blockTimestamp = event.block.timestamp
  entity.transactionHash = event.transaction.hash


  // Save the entity to the store
  entity.save()
}


/**
  * Add more event handlers as needed
  * Format: 
  * 
  * export function handleEventName(event: EventNameEvent): void {
  *   let entity = new EventName(
  *     event.transaction.hash.concatI32(event.logIndex.toI32())
  *   )
  *   
  *   // Map parameters
  *   entity.param1 = event.params.param1
  *   entity.param2 = event.params.param2
  *   
  *   // Add metadata
  *   entity.blockNumber = event.block.number
  *   entity.blockTimestamp = event.block.timestamp
  *   entity.transactionHash = event.transaction.hash
  *   
  *   entity.save()
  * }
  */

```

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/node-ops/full-node-configurations

Configuration options for the RaptorCast protocol mode, affecting participation in secondary RaptorCast and bandwidth usage. 'None' disables secondary participation, while 'Client' enables it and is required for prioritized and public full nodes.

}

RaptorCast {
  mode = "Client"
}

### Syscall: Reward Distribution

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Invoked for every block to reward the block-producing validator and their delegators, considering commission rates.

```APIDOC
## POST /syscallReward

### Description
This syscall is invoked for every block. It rewards the block-producing validator and their delegators with the configured block reward. If the validator has a nonzero commission, a portion is allocated to their `authAddress`, and the remainder is claimable by delegators.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/syscallReward

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
(none)

#### Query Parameters
(none)

#### Request Body

### Request Example

  "blockAuthor": "0xValidatorAddress"
}

### Response

(no specific response body documented, typically indicates successful execution of the syscall)

#### Response Example
(none)
```

### Calculate Gas Cost for SSTORE (Python)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/opcode-pricing

Calculates the gas cost for an SSTORE operation based on the new value, current value, and original value. This logic is derived from Ethereum's gas cost rules.

```python
if value == current_value:
    gas_cost = 100 (m)
elif current_value == original_value:
    if original_value == 0:
        gas_cost = 127900 (m + w + s)
    else:
        gas_cost = 2900 (m + w)
else:
    gas_cost = 100 (m)
```

### CatToken Solidity Deployment Script

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/indexers/ghost

A Foundry script to deploy the CatToken contract on the Monad Testnet. It reads the private key from the environment, broadcasts the deployment, and logs the deployed contract address.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

import "forge-std/Script.sol";
import "../src/CatToken.sol";

contract DeployCatToken is Script {
    function run() external {
        // Retrieve private key from environment
        uint256 deployerPrivateKey = vm.envUint("PRIVATE_KEY");

        vm.startBroadcast(deployerPrivateKey);
        CatToken token = new CatToken();
        vm.stopBroadcast();

        // Log the token address - this will be needed for GhostGraph indexing and submit transactions
        console.log("CatToken deployed to:", address(token));
    }
}
```

### POST /syscall/epochChange

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Triggers a system call for an epoch change, processing relevant updates.

```APIDOC
## POST /syscall/epochChange

### Description
Triggers a system call for an epoch change, processing relevant updates.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/syscall/epochChange

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **epoch** (uint64) - Required - The epoch number for which to trigger the change.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "epoch": 101
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- (No specific fields returned on success)

#### Response Example

```
```

### Mappings for Address to ID and State Tracking (Solidity)

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

Defines mappings to associate Ethereum addresses (SECP and BLS) with validator IDs and to store historical accumulator values and validator states. These mappings are essential for efficient data retrieval and state management.

```Solidity
//These mappings only exist to ensure the SECP/BLS Keys are unique
mapping (secp_eth_address => uint64) secp_to_val_id;
mapping (bls_eth_address => uint64) bls_to_val_id;


// Keys(val_id, epoch) => Value(acc)
// making note of the validator accumulator at start of epoch.
mapping(uint64 => mapping(uint64 => Accumulator)) epoch_acc;


// Key(val_id)
mapping(uint64 => ValExecution) val_execution;


// Key(val_id)
mapping(uint64 => ValConsensus) _val_consensus;


// Key(val_id)
mapping(uint64 => ValConsensus) _val_snapshot;


// Keys(val_id,msg.sender) => DelInfo
mapping(uint64 => mapping(address => DelInfo)) delegator;


// Keys(val_id,msg.sender,withdrawal_id) => WithdrawalRequest
mapping(uint64 => mapping(address => mapping (uint8 => WithdrawalRequest))) withdrawal;
```

### Compound Rewards - Pseudocode

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/staking/staking-precompile

This pseudocode illustrates the functionality of compounding a delegator's accumulated rewards into additional stake. It involves updating delegator stake, applying stake additions, and adjusting validator information. The function ensures that rewards are properly integrated into the active stake.

```pseudocode
validator_id = msg.input.val_id;


// set delegator information
DelInfo current_delegator = delegator[validator_id][msg.sender];


// apply get_current_stake() first. This updates the delegator stake
// to be inline with the current stake activated in consensus.
rewards_compounded = get_current_stake();


// apply add_stake() second.
uint256[4] add_stake_info = add_stake(rewards_compounded);


// set delegator information
current_delegator = DelInfo{
    uint256 delta_stake = add_stake_info[0];
    uint256 next_delta_stake = add_stake_info[1];
    uint64 delta_epoch = add_stake_info[2];
    uint64 next_delta_epoch = add_stake_info[3];
    uint256 rewards = 0;
}


// set validator information
val_execution[validator_id] = ValExecution{
    uint256 stake += rewards_compounded;
}


// set accumulator
epoch_acc[validator_id][getEpoch()] = Accumulator{
    uint256 ref_count += 1;
}


// set flags
set_flags();


// push validator id
if val_execution[validator_id].stake() >= ACTIVE_VALIDATOR_STAKE and validator_id not in execution_valset:
    execution_valset.push(validator_id);
```

### Configure actions.json for Blinks

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/blinks-guide

TypeScript code for a Next.js API route that configures `actions.json`. This file serves as a sitemap for blinks, mapping paths to API endpoints. It includes CORS headers for proper functionality.

```typescript
import { ACTIONS_CORS_HEADERS, ActionsJson } from "@solana/actions";


export const GET = async () => {
  const payload: ActionsJson = {
    rules: [
      // map all root level routes to an action
      {
        pathPattern: "/*",
        apiPath: "/api/actions/*",
      },
      // idempotent rule as the fallback
      {
        pathPattern: "/api/actions/**",
        apiPath: "/api/actions/**",
      },
    ],
  };


  return Response.json(payload, {
    headers: ACTIONS_CORS_HEADERS,
  });
};


// DO NOT FORGET TO INCLUDE THE `OPTIONS` HTTP METHOD
// THIS WILL ENSURE CORS WORKS FOR BLINKS
export const OPTIONS = GET;
```

### Monad Event Flow Tags

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/execution-events/event-ring

Explains the 'content_ext' fields and 'flow' tags used in Monad execution event rings for context and efficient data handling.

```APIDOC
## Monad Event Flow Tags

### Description
This section describes the use of `content_ext` fields within Monad execution event rings. These fields store additional data to help filter events and provide context, such as block sequence numbers, transaction IDs, and account indices.

### Content Extensions (`content_ext`)
- The `content_ext` array in an event descriptor stores additional data relevant to the event's content type.
- For execution event rings, the first three elements (`[0]`, `[1]`, `[2]`) have specific semantic meanings defined by the `monad_exec_flow_type` enumeration.

### `monad_exec_flow_type` Enumeration
This enumeration defines the semantic meaning of values stored in the `content_ext` array for execution events.

- **MONAD_FLOW_BLOCK_SEQNO (0)**: Represents the sequence number of the `BLOCK_START` event associated with the current block. This is used for block identification and compression. It can be zero for events not associated with a block (e.g., consensus state change events).
- **MONAD_FLOW_TXN_ID (1)**: Represents the transaction ID, which is the transaction index plus one. It is zero if the event is not associated with a transaction (e.g., at the start of a new block).
- **MONAD_FLOW_ACCOUNT_INDEX (2)**: Represents the index of the account involved in the event, typically related to an earlier `ACCOUNT_ACCESS` event. This is used for compression, as full account addresses are large.

### Usage Examples

#### Storage Access Event
For a `STORAGE_ACCESS` event:
- `event.content_ext[MONAD_FLOW_TXN_ID] - 1` provides the index of the transaction that performed the access.
- `event.content_ext[MONAD_FLOW_ACCOUNT_INDEX]` provides the index of the account storage key that was accessed.

#### Block Association
For events associated with a block:
- `event.content_ext[MONAD_FLOW_BLOCK_SEQNO]` stores the sequence number of the `BLOCK_START` event. This is a compressed representation of the block's unique identifier, used because block hashes are large and block status (proposed vs. finalized) can change.

### Benefits of Flow Tags

1.  **Fast Filtering**: Flow tags allow for rapid filtering of events based on transaction IDs or other contextual information, crucial for high-throughput systems where processing time per event is limited. A boolean array can be used for efficient lookup of relevant transaction IDs.
2.  **Compression**: Flow tags compress large data like account addresses and block identifiers into smaller integer indices, saving space and improving performance by reducing memory access latency.

### Request Example
No direct API requests are made in this context; this describes internal event data structures.

### Response Example
Illustrative content of `event.content_ext` for a `STORAGE_ACCESS` event within a block:

```json
{
  "event_type": "STORAGE_ACCESS",
  "content_ext": [
    3,  // MONAD_FLOW_BLOCK_SEQNO (Example: Sequence number of the block start event)
    5,  // MONAD_FLOW_TXN_ID (Example: Transaction ID = 4 + 1)
    2   // MONAD_FLOW_ACCOUNT_INDEX (Example: Index of the accessed account)
  ],
  "payload": { ... } // Actual event data
}
```
```

const config: HardhatUserConfig = {
  solidity: {
    version: "0.8.28",

        useLiteralContent: true, // use source code
      },
    },
  },

      url: "https://testnet-rpc.monad.xyz",
      chainId: 10143,
    },
  },
  
  sourcify: {
    enabled: true,
    apiUrl: "https://sourcify-api-monad.blockvision.org",
    browserUrl: "https://testnet.monadexplorer.com",
  },
  // To avoid errors from Etherscan
  etherscan: {
    enabled: false,
  },
};


export default config;
```

### Telegram Bot and Explorer Constants

Source: https://docs.monad.xyz/guides/indexers/tg-bot-using-envio

This TypeScript file defines constants used for interacting with the Telegram Bot API and Monad Explorer. It includes the base URL for the Monad Explorer, a threshold for WMON transfer amounts, Telegram bot token and chat ID obtained from environment variables, and helper functions to generate explorer URLs for addresses and transaction hashes.

```typescript
export const EXPLORER_URL_MONAD = "https://testnet.monadexplorer.com/";


// Threshold for WMON transfer amount above which the bot sends a notification
export const THRESHOLD_WEI: string = process.env.ENVIO_THRESHOLD_WEI ?? "1000000000000000000"; // in wei


export const BOT_TOKEN = process.env.ENVIO_BOT_TOKEN; // Telegram bot token
export const CHAT_ID = process.env.ENVIO_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID; // WMON Transfers Notification Channel ID


// Function to get explorer url for the provided address
export const explorerUrlAddress = (address: string) =>
  EXPLORER_URL_MONAD + "address/" + address;


// Function to get explorer url for the provided transaction hash
export const explorerUrlTx = (txHash: string) =>
  EXPLORER_URL_MONAD + "tx/" + txHash;

```
